    * **Algorithm Design:** The two-stage algorithm provides a practical approach to solving the complex optimization problem. The use of SDR and SCA is appropriate, and the heuristics for parameter tuning are helpful.
    * **Theoretical Analysis:** The convergence proof provides a theoretical foundation for the algorithm's performance.
    * **Experimental Validation:**  The simulation results demonstrate the effectiveness of the proposed framework, showing improvements over relevant benchmark schemes in terms of MSE, Perplexity, and Generation time. Results with increasing number of devices also shows the benefits of using the proposed scheme in larger networks. Use of open-source LLMs and a real-world dataset enhances reproducibility.
    * **Multi-antenna Extension:** Extending the framework to multi-antenna devices is a valuable addition that enhances the practical relevance of the proposed method.
* **Weaknesses:**
    * **Synchronization assumptions:** The AirComp approach relies on precise symbol-level synchronization, which can be challenging to achieve in practice. The paper mentions the use of TA mechanism but could further discuss the impact of imperfect synchronization.
    * **Channel model assumptions:** The Rician fading channel model, while common, may not accurately represent all real-world wireless environments. The robustness of the framework to other channel models should be investigated.
    * **Limited hardware Validation:** The experiments rely primarily on simulations.  Validating the performance of the framework on real-world edge devices would significantly strengthen the results.
    * **Scalability consideration:** While the simulations showcase performance improvement in large networks, scalability of AirComp solution is also critical. Overheads of large networks can be discussed further.

* **Overall Assessment:** The paper makes a valuable contribution to the field of distributed LLM inference. The combination of tensor parallelism, AirComp, and joint optimization is novel and well-executed. The theoretical analysis and simulation results provide a solid foundation for the proposed framework. While there are limitations related to synchronization, channel modeling and validation on real hardware, the overall approach is sound and warrants recognition for advancing this area.

**Score: 7.5**

**Rationale:** The paper presents a novel approach to an important problem, backed by strong theoretical and empirical results. The weaknesses, while noteworthy, do not detract significantly from the overall contribution. The framework could provide practical benefits for deploying LLMs on edge devices and improve the efficiency and accuracy of LLM inference. Future research should focus on addressing the limitations mentioned above to further enhance the practical applicability of the proposed framework. The work's potential impact on the field warrants a score of 7.5

- **Classification**: cs.DC
- **Score**: 7/10

### Envisioning an AI-Enhanced Mental Health Ecosystem
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.14883v1)
- **Authors**: Kellie Yu Hui Sim, Kenny Tsu Wei Choo
- **Abstract**: The rapid advancement of Large Language Models (LLMs), reasoning models, and agentic AI approaches coincides with a growing global mental health crisis, where increasing demand has not translated into adequate access to professional support, particularly for underserved populations. This presents a unique opportunity for AI to complement human-led interventions, offering scalable and context-aware support while preserving human connection in this sensitive domain. We explore various AI applications in peer support, self-help interventions, proactive monitoring, and data-driven insights, using a human-centred approach that ensures AI supports rather than replaces human interaction. However, AI deployment in mental health fields presents challenges such as ethical concerns, transparency, privacy risks, and risks of over-reliance. We propose a hybrid ecosystem where where AI assists but does not replace human providers, emphasising responsible deployment and evaluation. We also present some of our early work and findings in several of these AI applications. Finally, we outline future research directions for refining AI-enhanced interventions while adhering to ethical and culturally sensitive guidelines.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper "Envisioning an AI-Enhanced Mental Health Ecosystem" discusses the potential of AI, particularly Large Language Models (LLMs) and agentic AI, to address the growing global mental health crisis. It argues that AI can complement human-led interventions by offering scalable and context-aware support in areas like peer support, self-help, proactive monitoring, and data-driven insights. The authors emphasize a human-centered approach, ensuring AI assists rather than replaces human interaction. They address ethical concerns, transparency, privacy, and over-reliance risks, proposing a hybrid ecosystem.  The paper also presents preliminary work and outlines future research directions for refining AI-enhanced interventions, adhering to ethical and culturally sensitive guidelines. It explores various AI applications, including AI-simulated clients, AI as a peer counselor, AI-generated suggestions, AI for decision support, AI for self-help, proactive monitoring, and AI as an embedded companion. It concludes by proposing ways to integrate these various AI applications.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Strengths:**
    * **Comprehensive Scope:** The paper provides a broad overview of various AI applications in mental health, covering many potential use cases.
    * **Emphasis on Human-Centered Design:**  The consistent focus on a human-centered approach is a major strength.  It acknowledges the limitations of AI and emphasizes the importance of maintaining human connection and oversight.
    * **Ethical Awareness:** The paper explicitly addresses crucial ethical concerns related to AI in mental health, including privacy, transparency, and potential biases.
    * **Practical Applications:**  The paper goes beyond theoretical discussions, describing preliminary work and suggesting concrete research directions.
    * **Ecosystem Vision:** The paper's envisioning of an integrated AI-enhanced mental health ecosystem is forward-thinking and highlights the potential for synergy between different AI approaches.

* **Weaknesses:**
    * **Limited Novelty:** The paper primarily presents a review and integration of existing ideas and research directions. While the comprehensive scope is valuable, it lacks groundbreaking novel contributions. It feels more like a position paper or high-level survey rather than a report of original research.
    * **Lack of Deep Dive:**  While the paper covers many topics, it doesn't delve deeply into the technical challenges or specific solutions for each application. The descriptions of the preliminary work are high-level.
    * **Over-reliance on LLM Capabilities:** The paper is somewhat optimistic about the current capabilities of LLMs in understanding complex emotional states and providing truly empathetic support.  While LLMs have made significant progress, their limitations in these areas are not fully addressed. The paper could acknowledge the limitations that LLMs hallucinate and provide non-helpful or incorrect information
    * **Limited Empirical Data:**  The paper mentions some studies and early work, but it lacks substantial empirical evidence to support the feasibility or effectiveness of the proposed ecosystem.
    * **Missing discussion on the cost:** The paper doesn't discuss the cost of setting up such a system.

* **Novelty and Significance:** The paper's novelty lies primarily in its integration of diverse AI applications into a cohesive ecosystem vision.  While individual AI applications have been explored, the holistic perspective and the emphasis on a human-centered approach are relatively novel.  The significance stems from the potential to improve access to mental health support and enhance the effectiveness of existing interventions. However, these potentials are not fully substantiated with evidence.

* **Potential Influence:** The paper could influence the field by encouraging researchers and practitioners to adopt a more integrated and ethical approach to AI in mental health. It provides a valuable framework for future research and development.

**Justification of Score:**

While the paper presents a valuable overview and a forward-looking vision, its lack of original research findings and the relatively high-level nature of its discussion limit its overall impact. The identified weaknesses, particularly the lack of deep dives and the optimistic view of LLM capabilities, also detract from its overall strength. The significance is primarily in suggesting a direction for future integration, rather than providing concrete advancements.

Score: 6

- **Classification**: cs.HC
- **Score**: 6/10

### Pseudo-Relevance Feedback Can Improve Zero-Shot LLM-Based Dense Retrieval
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.14887v1)
- **Authors**: Hang Li, Xiao Wang, Bevan Koopman, Guido Zuccon
- **Abstract**: Pseudo-relevance feedback (PRF) refines queries by leveraging initially retrieved documents to improve retrieval effectiveness. In this paper, we investigate how large language models (LLMs) can facilitate PRF for zero-shot LLM-based dense retrieval, extending the recently proposed PromptReps method. Specifically, our approach uses LLMs to extract salient passage features-such as keywords and summaries-from top-ranked documents, which are then integrated into PromptReps to produce enhanced query representations. Experiments on passage retrieval benchmarks demonstrate that incorporating PRF significantly boosts retrieval performance. Notably, smaller rankers with PRF can match the effectiveness of larger rankers without PRF, highlighting PRF's potential to improve LLM-driven search while maintaining an efficient balance between effectiveness and resource usage.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper "Pseudo-Relevance Feedback Can Improve Zero-Shot LLM-Based Dense Retrieval" explores how to enhance zero-shot dense retrieval using Pseudo-Relevance Feedback (PRF) within the PromptReps framework. It introduces PromptPRF, a novel approach that leverages LLMs to extract salient passage features (keywords, summaries, etc.) from top-ranked documents retrieved in an initial retrieval stage. These extracted features, along with rank information, are integrated into PromptReps prompts to refine the query representation. The updated query representation is then used in a second retrieval stage. Experiments on TREC DL passage retrieval tasks demonstrate that PromptPRF significantly improves retrieval performance, allowing smaller rankers to achieve effectiveness comparable to larger models without PRF. The authors also explore the impact of different feature extractors, feature types, and PRF depths.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The key novelty lies in the specific way PRF is implemented within the zero-shot PromptReps framework. The idea of using LLMs to extract features for PRF isn't entirely new, but the prompts specifically designed to extract features and how these features are integrated into a new query representation within the PromptReps approach is unique. The focus on zero-shot retrieval and the practical implications of using smaller rankers for efficiency further adds to the novelty. The paper's novelty is not ground-breaking, as other methods have used LLMs for query expansion, but the combination of techniques and the specific prompt engineering distinguishes it.

*   **Significance:** The significance stems from the potential to improve the efficiency of LLM-based retrieval. The finding that smaller rankers with PRF can match larger rankers without PRF is important because it suggests a path towards more resource-efficient retrieval systems. This is especially relevant given the computational cost of large LLMs. The exploration of different feature types and PRF depths also provides valuable insights for optimizing PRF strategies. However, the observed gains, while statistically significant, may not always be practically substantial in real-world applications.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   The method is simple to implement and doesn't require additional training, aligning well with the zero-shot nature of PromptReps.
    *   The experimental results demonstrate significant improvements on standard passage retrieval benchmarks.
    *   The analysis of the impact of different feature extractors, feature types, and PRF depths is thorough and insightful.
    *   The discussion of the trade-offs between effectiveness and efficiency is well-reasoned.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   The approach relies heavily on the quality of the initial retrieval and the LLM-generated features.
    *   The parameters of the model such as the influence of each passage's extracted features weren't studied.
    *   The impact of different feature types could be more comprehensively studied, since they did not include an experiment on the best combination of features.
    *   The prompts used for feature extraction are important, but their details were not shared in the paper itself.
    *   The improvement from smaller rankers may not translate to larger datasets.

*   **Justification for Score:** The paper presents a solid, incremental contribution to the field of LLM-based retrieval. While the core idea of using LLMs for PRF isn't entirely new, the specific implementation within PromptReps and the focus on zero-shot retrieval with resource efficiency is valuable. The experimental results support the claims, and the analysis provides useful insights. However, the limitations related to the reliance on initial retrieval quality, potentially small real-world performance gains, and reliance on careful prompt engineering prevent it from being a truly groundbreaking contribution.

**Score: 6.5**
- **Classification**: cs.IR
- **Score**: 6/10

### MetaLadder: Ascending Mathematical Solution Quality via Analogical-Problem Reasoning Transfer
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.14891v1)
- **Authors**: Honglin Lin, Zhuoshi Pan, Yu Li, Qizhi Pei, Xin Gao, Mengzhang Cai, Conghui He, Lijun Wu
- **Abstract**: Large Language Models (LLMs) have demonstrated promising capabilities in solving mathematical reasoning tasks, leveraging Chain-of-Thought (CoT) data as a vital component in guiding answer generation. Current paradigms typically generate CoT and answers directly for a given problem, diverging from human problem-solving strategies to some extent. Humans often solve problems by recalling analogous cases and leveraging their solutions to reason about the current task. Inspired by this cognitive process, we propose \textbf{MetaLadder}, a novel framework that explicitly prompts LLMs to recall and reflect on meta-problems, those structurally or semantically analogous problems, alongside their CoT solutions before addressing the target problem. Additionally, we introduce a problem-restating mechanism to enhance the model's comprehension of the target problem by regenerating the original question, which further improves reasoning accuracy. Therefore, the model can achieve reasoning transfer from analogical problems, mimicking human-like "learning from examples" and generalization abilities. Extensive experiments on mathematical benchmarks demonstrate that our MetaLadder significantly boosts LLMs' problem-solving accuracy, largely outperforming standard CoT-based methods (\textbf{10.3\%} accuracy gain) and other methods. Our code and data has been released at https://github.com/LHL3341/MetaLadder.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces MetaLadder, a novel framework designed to enhance the mathematical reasoning capabilities of Large Language Models (LLMs).  The core idea is to augment training data with examples of "meta-problems" – structurally or semantically analogous problems along with their solutions – before presenting the target problem. This mimics human problem-solving strategies where previous experiences with similar problems are leveraged.  The framework also incorporates a problem-restating mechanism where the LLM rephrases the original question to improve comprehension.  Experiments on mathematical benchmarks (GSM8K and MATH) show that MetaLadder significantly improves problem-solving accuracy compared to standard Chain-of-Thought (CoT) methods and other augmentation strategies. A key finding is that this approach improves generalization to out-of-distribution problems.  The paper also explores a self-evolution process where the LLM generates its own analogous problems for further training. A "shortcut inference" method is also presented, which bypasses the explicit generation of analogous problems during inference for faster processing.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:** The core idea of incorporating analogical reasoning into LLM training for mathematical tasks is a significant and well-motivated contribution. While RAG and data augmentation techniques exist, this is a unique approach that aligns more closely with cognitive processes observed in human problem-solving. The combination of analogical recall and problem restatement is also innovative. The self-evolution idea, while explored in other contexts, is effectively adapted here. The Shortcut Inference method is a clever optimization that helps demonstrate how the benefits of the analogical data can be leveraged at test time without its added expense.

**Significance:** The results demonstrate a substantial improvement in accuracy on both in-domain and out-of-domain benchmarks, indicating a stronger generalization ability. The potential impact on mathematical problem-solving with LLMs is substantial, as the gains are achieved without significant increases in model size. The demonstrated improvement in out-of-distribution tasks is particularly important, as it addresses a key limitation of many current approaches. The "self-evolution" idea offers an interesting avenue for continuous model improvement and efficient data augmentation. The shortcut inference method enables faster application.

**Strengths:**

*   **Clear Motivation:** The paper effectively argues for the importance of analogical reasoning in mathematical problem-solving and convincingly explains how MetaLadder addresses this aspect.
*   **Well-Defined Framework:** MetaLadder is clearly defined with well-articulated components (analogical problem recall, problem restatement, shortcut inference).
*   **Comprehensive Evaluation:**  The experimental setup is thorough, using multiple benchmarks and comparing against strong baselines, including other augmentation methods and state-of-the-art techniques. The use of both in-domain and out-of-domain datasets is a notable strength.
*   **Ablation Studies:**  The ablation studies provide insights into the contribution of individual components, highlighting the importance of each aspect of the MetaLadder framework.
*   **Case Study:**  The case study offers a qualitative understanding of how MetaLadder facilitates more structured and generalizable problem-solving.
*   **Self-evolution and Shortcut inference:** The self-evolution and shortcut inference results add a great deal of value to the paper, demonstrating the adaptablity and efficiency of the model.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Data Generation Dependence:** The framework's performance is tied to the quality of the "meta-problems" generated.  The paper mentions using GPT-40-mini for this purpose, but more detail on the prompt engineering and filtering process would be helpful.  Sensitivity analysis regarding the "quality" or "similarity" of the analogous problems and test of how the quality of the generation affects the overall outcome would make the paper stronger. Also, it should be noted that if GPT-4 is used, the gains are really showing the gains of that model not only of MetaLadder.
*   **Computational Cost of Training:**  The added complexity of generating and incorporating meta-problems inevitably increases the computational cost of training. While the "shortcut inference" mitigates this at inference time, the upfront cost might be a barrier to adoption. The paper offers little discussion as to that.
*   **Generalizability to other task types**: It is unclear if this method could be as effective in other tasks. While the framework attempts to make connections to the original questions, it might miss key connections that a human would have made.
*   **Ethical considerations:** There are no ethical considerations discussed in this work. Math problems might have unintended consequences that should be noted.

**Potential Influence:**

MetaLadder has the potential to influence research in several areas:

*   **LLM Training for Reasoning:**  It offers a promising alternative to standard CoT approaches by explicitly modeling analogical reasoning.
*   **Data Augmentation:**  The meta-problem generation strategy can be adapted for other tasks beyond mathematics.
*   **Cognitive Modeling:**  It provides a computational framework for exploring human problem-solving strategies.

**Justification for Score:**

Overall, this is a well-executed and valuable contribution to the field. The concept is novel, the results are significant, and the evaluation is thorough. The paper addresses a key limitation of existing LLM approaches and provides a practical framework for improving mathematical reasoning. While the data generation dependence and computational cost are valid concerns, the potential benefits outweigh these limitations. Therefore, the paper merits a strong score.

Score: 8

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 8/10

### Mitigating Object Hallucinations in MLLMs via Multi-Frequency Perturbations
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.14895v1)
- **Authors**: Shuo Li, Jiajun Sun, Guodong Zheng, Xiaoran Fan, Yujiong Shen, Yi Lu, Zhiheng Xi, Yuming Yang, Wenming Tan, Tao Ji, Tao Gui, Qi Zhang, Xuanjing Huang
- **Abstract**: Recently, multimodal large language models (MLLMs) have demonstrated remarkable performance in visual-language tasks. However, the authenticity of the responses generated by MLLMs is often compromised by object hallucinations. We identify that a key cause of these hallucinations is the model's over-susceptibility to specific image frequency features in detecting objects. In this paper, we introduce Multi-Frequency Perturbations (MFP), a simple, cost-effective, and pluggable method that leverages both low-frequency and high-frequency features of images to perturb visual feature representations and explicitly suppress redundant frequency-domain features during inference, thereby mitigating hallucinations. Experimental results demonstrate that our method significantly mitigates object hallucinations across various model architectures. Furthermore, as a training-time method, MFP can be combined with inference-time methods to achieve state-of-the-art performance on the CHAIR benchmark.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper "Mitigating Object Hallucinations in MLLMs via Multi-Frequency Perturbations" addresses the problem of object hallucinations in Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs). The authors hypothesize that MLLMs are overly susceptible to specific image frequency features (both low and high) when detecting objects, leading to incorrect object detection and hallucinations. To combat this, they propose Multi-Frequency Perturbations (MFP), a simple, cost-effective, and pluggable method. MFP extracts both low- and high-frequency features from the image and fuses them with the original image features at the visual token level. During inference, a decay is applied to both high- and low-frequency feature perturbations to reduce redundant high-frequency features. The experimental results demonstrate that MFP effectively mitigates object hallucinations across various model architectures and can be combined with existing inference-time methods to achieve state-of-the-art performance on the CHAIR benchmark.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Strengths:**

*   **Clear Problem Definition:** The paper clearly identifies and defines the problem of object hallucination in MLLMs.
*   **Novel Hypothesis:** The paper introduces a novel and interesting hypothesis regarding the role of image frequency features in causing object hallucinations in MLLMs. While frequency domain attacks have been studied in other contexts, this paper is the first to evaluate frequency domain features within MLLMs.
*   **Simple and Effective Method:** The proposed MFP method is simple, cost-effective, and pluggable, making it easy to implement and integrate with existing MLLM architectures.
*   **Comprehensive Evaluation:** The paper presents a thorough evaluation of MFP across various model architectures, benchmarks, and settings.
*   **State-of-the-art Results:** The paper demonstrates that MFP can achieve state-of-the-art performance on the CHAIR benchmark, indicating its effectiveness in mitigating object hallucinations.
*   **Good Ablation Studies:** The paper performs an ablation study to understand the individual contribution of components within MFP.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Incremental Improvement:** The approach leverages known techniques for frequency based feature extraction and fusion. The novelty stems primarily from the application of these techniques to mitigate hallucinations in MLLMs. While the method yields improvements, the gains aren't revolutionary, but rather a solid, incremental step.
*   **Limited Generalization Analysis:** While the paper shows some evidence of cross-architecture generalization, a more in-depth analysis of how MFP performs on more diverse datasets and complex scenarios would be beneficial. Does MFP perform equally well on images with complex backgrounds, occlusions, or varying lighting conditions?
*   **Hyperparameter Sensitivity:** The sensitivity analysis focuses only on the attenuation factor. A more comprehensive analysis of other hyperparameters, such as the cutoff frequency for the Gaussian filters, might reveal further insights into the method's robustness and optimal configuration.
*   **Justification for frequency domain manipulation:** While the paper observes that MLLMs can identify images even when certain frequencies are used, it doesn't *fully* explain *why* this makes them susceptible to hallucinations, or *why* suppressing specific frequency features solves this problem. The justification could be strengthened with a theoretical argument or a more detailed analysis of the model's internal representations.

**Novelty and Significance:**

The paper's primary novelty lies in its hypothesis that MLLMs are overly susceptible to frequency-domain features, and its proposed MFP method leverages this understanding to mitigate object hallucinations. This is a significant contribution because it offers a new perspective on a long-standing problem in MLLMs. The paper also demonstrates that MFP can be combined with existing methods to achieve state-of-the-art performance. The frequency-based approach provides a fresh direction for future research on hallucination mitigation.

**Score:** 7

**Justification:**

The paper presents a novel perspective on object hallucinations in MLLMs and proposes a simple, effective, and pluggable method to address this problem. The experimental results are compelling, and the paper makes a valuable contribution to the field. While the improvement in some benchmarks can be seen as incremental, it is a clear and consistent improvement achieved with a principled approach. However, the paper has some limitations, such as its focus on a limited set of model architectures, the lack of theoretical justification for the method, and the sensitivity to some hyper parameters. It introduces a fresh perspective that is backed with quantitative analysis, but it could benefit from greater theoretical depth and a comprehensive sensitivity analysis. Given the paper's strengths and weaknesses, it is assigned a score of 7, indicating a solid contribution to the field with potential for further development and impact.

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### Deep Contrastive Unlearning for Language Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.14900v1)
- **Authors**: Estrid He, Tabinda Sarwar, Ibrahim Khalil, Xun Yi, Ke Wang
- **Abstract**: The past a few years have witnessed the great success of large language models, demonstrating powerful capabilities in comprehending textual data and generating human-like languages. Large language models achieve success by being trained on vast amounts of textual data, including online sources with copyrighted content and user-generated knowledge. However, this comes at a cost: the potential risk of exposing users' privacy and violating copyright protections. Thus, to safeguard individuals' "right to be forgotten", there has been increasing interests in machine unlearning -- the process of removing information carried by particular training samples from a model while not deteriorating its predictive quality. This is a challenging task due to the black-box nature of language models. Most existing studies focus on mitigating the impact of those forgot samples upon a model's outputs, and do not explicitly consider the geometric distributions of samples in the latent space of a model. To address this issue, we propose a machine unlearning framework, named Deep Contrastive Unlearning for fine-Tuning (DeepCUT) language models. Our proposed model achieves machine unlearning by directly optimizing the latent space of a model. Comprehensive experiments on real-world datasets demonstrate the effectiveness and efficiency of DeepCUT with consistent and significant improvement over baseline methods.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper, aiming for a rigorous assessment:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces Deep Contrastive Unlearning for fine-Tuning (DeepCUT), a novel framework for machine unlearning in language models. Addressing concerns about privacy and the "right to be forgotten" amidst the widespread use of large language models (LLMs), DeepCUT focuses on directly manipulating the latent space of a fine-tuned language model. Instead of solely mitigating the impact of forgotten samples on the model's outputs, DeepCUT optimizes the geometric representation of data by pushing "anchor samples" (data to be forgotten) away from samples of the same class and closer to samples of different classes. This aims to remove discriminative features specific to the forgotten samples. The authors demonstrate the effectiveness and efficiency of DeepCUT across various datasets and compare it against several baselines.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:** The central idea of directly manipulating the latent space using contrastive learning for machine unlearning is where the paper gains its novelty. While contrastive learning has been applied in various NLP tasks, its application for machine unlearning, particularly by focusing on the geometric distribution of data in the latent space, is relatively novel. This differs from prior approaches that primarily focus on output probability distribution or use more generic adversarial forgetting methods.  The data augmentation strategy employing dropout in the latent space also contributes to the novelty.  However, using dropout for data augmentation is not novel *per se*; the specific application within the DeepCUT framework and its impact on unlearning is the key point. It appears to be a good application. The technique is technically well-motivated but the application is incrementally novel.

**Significance:** The significance of this work lies in addressing a crucial challenge related to the ethical deployment of LLMs – data privacy and the right to be forgotten. The paper provides a practical approach to unlearning that is both effective and efficient.  The empirical results show a consistent improvement over existing unlearning methods, highlighting the potential of DeepCUT. By focusing on the geometric distributions in the latent space, the method could lead to more precise and targeted unlearning, minimizing the negative impact on the overall performance of the model. The approach is general, such that it can be extended and adopted. The comprehensive experimental evaluation and the inclusion of baselines are strong.

**Strengths:**

*   **Clear Problem Definition:** The paper clearly articulates the problem of machine unlearning in the context of LLMs and privacy concerns.
*   **Novel Approach:** The contrastive learning-based approach for latent space manipulation is a significant contribution.
*   **Strong Empirical Results:** The experimental results consistently demonstrate the superiority of DeepCUT over baseline methods, particularly in unlearning effectiveness.
*   **Comprehensive Evaluation:** The paper evaluates the approach across different datasets and metrics (unlearning effectiveness, predictive performance, and algorithm efficiency), providing a holistic assessment.
*   **Well-Explained Methodology:** The DeepCUT framework is clearly explained and well-motivated.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Limited Theoretical Analysis:** While the paper provides a practical solution, it lacks in-depth theoretical analysis on the properties of the latent space manipulation and its impact on the model's generalization ability. The guarantees of the approach is lacking.
*   **Hyperparameter Sensitivity:** The performance of contrastive learning methods is often sensitive to the choice of hyperparameters like temperature (τ) and unlearning tradeoff parameter (λ). Although the paper does mention searching for optimal values, a deeper discussion on the sensitivity and robustness of the method to hyperparameter settings could be beneficial.
*   **Scalability Considerations:** While the paper demonstrates efficiency compared to retraining, the scalability of DeepCUT to extremely large models and datasets could be a concern. Future work should address this aspect. It is important to mention the limitations of the scalability.

**Potential Influence:**

This work could have a significant impact on the field of machine unlearning for LLMs.  By directly optimizing the latent space, DeepCUT offers a promising direction for developing more effective and efficient unlearning techniques. The approach could inspire further research on leveraging contrastive learning and other representation learning techniques for privacy-preserving machine learning. The open-source availability of the DeepCUT implementation would further facilitate adoption and further research.

**Rigorous Rationale for the Score:**

DeepCUT introduces a reasonably novel approach to machine unlearning by leveraging contrastive learning in the latent space of language models. While the individual components of the method (contrastive learning, data augmentation) are not entirely new, their combination and specific application to the unlearning problem are. The empirical results provide solid evidence of DeepCUT's effectiveness, consistently outperforming several baselines. The paper addresses a timely and important problem.

However, there are areas for improvement. The theoretical analysis is somewhat lacking, and a deeper understanding of the latent space manipulation is needed.  The hyperparameter sensitivity of the method should also be addressed. Also, the degree of the novelty of the application of dropout augmentation is not very strong.

Considering the novelty, significance, strengths, and weaknesses, a score of 7 is justified. It offers a valuable contribution to the field with clear practical benefits, but has room for further theoretical development and scalability improvements.

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### FetalFlex: Anatomy-Guided Diffusion Model for Flexible Control on Fetal Ultrasound Image Synthesis
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.14906v1)
- **Authors**: Yaofei Duan, Tao Tan, Zhiyuan Zhu, Yuhao Huang, Yuanji Zhang, Rui Gao, Patrick Cheong-Iao Pang, Xinru Gao, Guowei Tao, Xiang Cong, Zhou Li, Lianying Liang, Guangzhi He, Linliang Yin, Xuedong Deng, Xin Yang, Dong Ni
- **Abstract**: Fetal ultrasound (US) examinations require the acquisition of multiple planes, each providing unique diagnostic information to evaluate fetal development and screening for congenital anomalies. However, obtaining a comprehensive, multi-plane annotated fetal US dataset remains challenging, particularly for rare or complex anomalies owing to their low incidence and numerous subtypes. This poses difficulties in training novice radiologists and developing robust AI models, especially for detecting abnormal fetuses. In this study, we introduce a Flexible Fetal US image generation framework (FetalFlex) to address these challenges, which leverages anatomical structures and multimodal information to enable controllable synthesis of fetal US images across diverse planes. Specifically, FetalFlex incorporates a pre-alignment module to enhance controllability and introduces a repaint strategy to ensure consistent texture and appearance. Moreover, a two-stage adaptive sampling strategy is developed to progressively refine image quality from coarse to fine levels. We believe that FetalFlex is the first method capable of generating both in-distribution normal and out-of-distribution abnormal fetal US images, without requiring any abnormal data. Experiments on multi-center datasets demonstrate that FetalFlex achieved state-of-the-art performance across multiple image quality metrics. A reader study further confirms the close alignment of the generated results with expert visual assessments. Furthermore, synthetic images by FetalFlex significantly improve the performance of six typical deep models in downstream classification and anomaly detection tasks. Lastly, FetalFlex's anatomy-level controllable generation offers a unique advantage for anomaly simulation and creating paired or counterfactual data at the pixel level. The demo is available at: https://dyf1023.github.io/FetalFlex/.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided research paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces FetalFlex, a new anatomy-guided diffusion model designed for synthesizing fetal ultrasound (US) images.  It aims to address the challenges of limited and imbalanced datasets (particularly for rare anomalies) in fetal US imaging, which hinders the development of robust AI models. FetalFlex uses anatomical structures and multimodal information to enable controllable synthesis across different US planes.  Key features include a pre-alignment module for better anatomical control, a "repaint" strategy for consistent texture, and a two-stage adaptive sampling strategy to refine image quality.  The method can generate both normal and abnormal fetal US images without needing abnormal training data. Experiments across multiple datasets demonstrate state-of-the-art performance, reader studies confirm visual alignment with expert assessments, and synthetic images improve downstream task performance (classification and anomaly detection). FetalFlex enables anatomy-level control for anomaly simulation and paired/counterfactual data creation.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:**  The paper presents several novel components contributing to the overall approach:

*   **Anatomy-Guided Control:** Leveraging anatomical information derived from bounding box detections as control conditions is a well-motivated strategy. The image-layout pre-alignment module addresses the disparity in data distribution which adds to the novelty.
*   **Repaint Strategy with ROI masking:** Adapting the repaint strategy from natural images and incorporating a region of interest (ROI) is clever way to maintain consistency in a context where data is scarce. This demonstrates consideration of the unique characteristics of US imaging.
*   **Two-Stage Adaptive Sampling:**  The novel two-stage (SSA and OSM) sampling strategy is tailored to US imaging characteristics (acoustic impedance differences). This is one of the stronger contributions as it moves beyond general diffusion models towards a US-specific approach.
*   **Universal Model for Multiple Planes:**  The idea of using a single model for multiple fetal US planes is practical and addresses the limitations of training separate models for each plane.
*   **Anomaly Synthesis without Abnormal Data:** The ability to generate plausible abnormal cases *without* training on examples of those anomalies is a significant contribution, overcoming a major hurdle in this domain.

**Significance:**

*   **Addresses a real problem:**  The scarcity of annotated fetal US data, especially for rare anomalies, is a significant bottleneck in the field. FetalFlex directly tackles this problem.
*   **Performance:** Achieves state-of-the-art image quality in fetal US image synthesis, as supported by multiple metrics and reader studies.
*   **Impact on Downstream Tasks:** Demonstrates that synthetic images can improve the performance of existing deep learning models on key downstream tasks (classification, anomaly detection). This is a crucial step in demonstrating the practical value of the generated data.
*   **Controllability:** Offers a new level of control for generating and editing US images, opening up opportunities for training, education, and research. The ability to create paired/counterfactual data is particularly valuable.
*   **Clinical Utility:** The reader study is particularly strong, and necessary. While metrics are valuable the qualitative assessment by experts is essential to demonstrate clinical plausibility.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Dependency on Detection:** The framework relies on an initial object detection step (FTSPD). While this is claimed to be high accuracy, its performance is still an upper bound for the complete system. There may be improvements in end-to-end training, or alternative, simpler detection methods to eliminate this step.
*   **Limitations on abnormalities:** While the paper demonstrates impressive capabilities in anomaly generation, the real world anomalies are extremely diverse and complex and the paper is primarily limited to demonstrating specific pathologies (hydrocephalus, cleft palate). Additional future research will be needed to address these diverse challenges.
*   **Data Availability:** The model is not truly free to operate from any pre-existing US. The detection and layout maps have to be pre-existing or automatically extracted. Additional future research should focus on how to eliminate this bottleneck.

**Justification of Score:**

The paper offers significant novelty and addresses a critical problem in the field of fetal US image analysis. The results are strong, and the controllability offered by the method has the potential to significantly impact training, education, and research efforts. However, the dependency on initial object detection does limit the approach. The paper convincingly demonstrated visual plausibility of normal US images and some evidence for generated anomalies. As such, I assign a score of 8, reflecting a significant contribution that moves the field forward while acknowledging some limitations and areas for future research.

**Score: 8**

- **Classification**: eess.IV
- **Score**: 8/10

### POSTA: A Go-to Framework for Customized Artistic Poster Generation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.14908v1)
- **Authors**: Haoyu Chen, Xiaojie Xu, Wenbo Li, Jingjing Ren, Tian Ye, Songhua Liu, Ying-Cong Chen, Lei Zhu, Xinchao Wang
- **Abstract**: Poster design is a critical medium for visual communication. Prior work has explored automatic poster design using deep learning techniques, but these approaches lack text accuracy, user customization, and aesthetic appeal, limiting their applicability in artistic domains such as movies and exhibitions, where both clear content delivery and visual impact are essential. To address these limitations, we present POSTA: a modular framework powered by diffusion models and multimodal large language models (MLLMs) for customized artistic poster generation. The framework consists of three modules. Background Diffusion creates a themed background based on user input. Design MLLM then generates layout and typography elements that align with and complement the background style. Finally, to enhance the poster's aesthetic appeal, ArtText Diffusion applies additional stylization to key text elements. The final result is a visually cohesive and appealing poster, with a fully modular process that allows for complete customization. To train our models, we develop the PosterArt dataset, comprising high-quality artistic posters annotated with layout, typography, and pixel-level stylized text segmentation. Our comprehensive experimental analysis demonstrates POSTA's exceptional controllability and design diversity, outperforming existing models in both text accuracy and aesthetic quality.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the POSTA paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces POSTA, a modular framework for generating customized artistic posters using diffusion models and multimodal large language models (MLLMs). It addresses limitations of existing methods like inaccurate text generation, inflexible design control, and insufficient aesthetic quality.  POSTA comprises three main modules: Background Diffusion (generates themed backgrounds), Design MLLM (plans layout and typography), and ArtText Diffusion (applies artistic stylization to text).  A key contribution is the PosterArt dataset, containing high-quality artistic posters with annotations for layout, typography, and pixel-level stylized text, used for training the models.  The authors demonstrate that POSTA outperforms existing models in text accuracy, controllability, and aesthetic appeal through qualitative and quantitative evaluations.

**Critical Evaluation:**

The paper addresses a relevant and practically important problem: generating artistic and customizable posters. Existing methods often fall short in text accuracy, design control, or aesthetic appeal, limiting their real-world applicability. POSTA presents a systematic and modular workflow that attempts to address these issues, which is a notable strength.

*   **Novelty:** The modular approach, combining background generation, layout planning using MLLMs, and artistic text stylization, is reasonably novel. Using a dedicated dataset of artistic posters for training is also a plus. The specific implementation details (LoRA models, BrushNet-based inpainting) are perhaps less innovative individually, as they build on existing techniques, but their integration within the POSTA framework is what gives the contribution its merit.

*   **Significance:** The potential impact is significant. A robust, customizable poster generation tool could be valuable for graphic designers, marketers, and event organizers. The PosterArt dataset is a valuable contribution in itself, providing a resource for training and evaluating poster generation models. The claim that the entire workflow aligns with the pipeline of professional designers is also a very promising point.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Modularity:**  The modular architecture allows for easier customization and potential replacement of individual components. This improves adaptability.
    *   **Text Accuracy:** Addressing the text accuracy problem is crucial for poster generation, and POSTA seems to achieve significant improvements in this area. Direct text rendering based on typography prediction is the key to this accurate text handling.
    *   **Control and Customization:** The fine-grained control over design elements (background, layout, font, style) is a major advantage.
    *   **Dataset:** The PosterArt dataset is a significant contribution to the field, providing a valuable resource for training and evaluation, in particular concerning artistic styles.
    *   **Evaluations:** Extensive qualitative and quantitative evaluations demonstrate the superiority of POSTA over existing methods.
*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limitations on Layout Design:** The claim that the Design MLLM is "limited to generating relatively simple layout designs" is concerning. This is quite limiting considering the objective of the work. Complex layouts might be a crucial factor for generating outstanding artistic posters, so more effort has to be put into tackling complex layouts.
    *   **Limited Font Selection:** Similarly, a restricted selection of font types might limit design diversity. Even with artistic text stylization, starting with a limited selection seems to reduce the framework's potential impact.
    *   **Dependency on GPT-4V:** Relying on GPT-4V (a closed-source API) for prompt refinement and description extraction introduces a dependency and may limit reproducibility for some researchers. While using GPT-4V improves quality, it needs to be carefully considered, especially for the design planning module which is critical for the entire framework.

*   **Justification of Score:**

While the paper presents a valuable framework with impressive results and a useful dataset, the limitations regarding layout complexity and font selection hold it back from being truly groundbreaking. The reliance on a closed-source API also detracts slightly from its accessibility. However, the modular approach, text accuracy improvements, and overall aesthetic quality demonstrate a significant advancement in the field. The work also contributes a curated dataset to the research community.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.GR
- **Score**: 7/10

### MASS: Mathematical Data Selection via Skill Graphs for Pretraining Large Language Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.14917v1)
- **Authors**: Jiazheng Li, Lu Yu, Qing Cui, Zhiqiang Zhang, Jun Zhou, Yanfang Ye, Chuxu Zhang
- **Abstract**: High-quality data plays a critical role in the pretraining and fine-tuning of large language models (LLMs), even determining their performance ceiling to some degree. Consequently, numerous data selection methods have been proposed to identify subsets of data that can effectively and efficiently enhance model performance. However, most of these methods focus on general data selection and tend to overlook the specific nuances of domain-related data. In this paper, we introduce MASS, a \textbf{MA}thematical data \textbf{S}election framework using the \textbf{S}kill graph for pretraining LLMs in the mathematical reasoning domain. By taking into account the unique characteristics of mathematics and reasoning, we construct a skill graph that captures the mathematical skills and their interrelations from a reference dataset. This skill graph guides us in assigning quality scores to the target dataset, enabling us to select the top-ranked subset which is further used to pretrain LLMs. Experimental results demonstrate the efficiency and effectiveness of MASS across different model sizes (1B and 7B) and pretraining datasets (web data and synthetic data). Specifically, in terms of efficiency, models trained on subsets selected by MASS can achieve similar performance to models trained on the original datasets, with a significant reduction in the number of trained tokens - ranging from 50\% to 70\% fewer tokens. In terms of effectiveness, when trained on the same amount of tokens, models trained on the data selected by MASS outperform those trained on the original datasets by 3.3\% to 5.9\%. These results underscore the potential of MASS to improve both the efficiency and effectiveness of pretraining LLMs.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "MASS: MAthematical Data Selection via Skill Graphs for Pretraining Large Language Models":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces MASS (MAthematical data Selection), a novel framework for improving the mathematical reasoning abilities of large language models (LLMs). MASS uses a skill graph constructed from a high-quality reference dataset (NuminaMath) to guide data selection from a target dataset. The skill graph captures the mathematical skills and their interrelations. This graph is then used to score data points in the target dataset based on the relevance and coverage of skills, enabling the selection of a high-quality subset for pretraining LLMs.  Experiments with TinyLlama-1.1B and Mistral-7B demonstrate that pretraining on the MASS-selected subsets leads to either comparable performance with significantly fewer training tokens or superior performance with the same number of tokens compared to training on the original datasets.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies in its *skill-graph-guided approach* to data selection *specifically for mathematical reasoning*. While data selection methods are not new, MASS distinguishes itself by incorporating the *structure of mathematical skills and their dependencies* into the selection process. This goes beyond simple heuristic or model-based scoring methods that treat data in a domain-agnostic fashion. The idea of using a skill graph to assess data quality is innovative, addressing a gap in current data selection methodologies that often overlook domain-specific nuances. However, the underlying idea of using a reference dataset to inform target data selection isn't completely new. Also, the reliance on a strong LLM (Qwen2.5-72B) to extract the initial skills introduces a dependence on existing model performance, which can be viewed as a limitation to the method's true self-sufficiency.

*   **Significance:** The potential significance of this work is in enabling more *efficient and effective pretraining* for LLMs in the mathematical domain. By identifying high-quality data subsets, MASS reduces the computational burden of pretraining and potentially improves the performance on downstream mathematical tasks. The reported performance improvements (3.3% to 5.9%) and token reduction (50% to 70%) are promising, indicating that MASS can be a valuable tool for practitioners looking to enhance LLMs' mathematical reasoning capabilities. However, the performance gain and token reduction achieved by MASS over the original datasets may not be significant enough to warrant its complexity of implementation.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   A clear and well-defined framework.
    *   Strong experimental results demonstrate efficiency and effectiveness.
    *   Ablation studies provide insights into the importance of different components.
    *   Detailed analysis of results, including data quality and selection ratio impact.
    *   Addresses a domain-specific gap in existing data selection methods.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   Dependence on the quality of the reference dataset (NuminaMath) and the pre-existing LLM (Qwen2.5-72B) used for skill extraction. The quality of the skill graph is directly linked to the initial skill extraction.
    *   The method is computationally intensive due to reliance on similarity computations and graph operations, although this is partially mitigated by matrix multiplication optimizations. The process of constructing the skill graph adds to the computational complexity.
    *   Limited generalizability: The skill graph construction and weighting might require careful adaptation for other domains, reducing its plug-and-play nature.
    *   The performance gain and token reduction achieved by MASS over the original datasets may not be significant enough to warrant its complexity of implementation.
    *   While the experiments show promising results on the chosen datasets and models, the evaluation could benefit from broader validation. Applying MASS to other mathematical datasets, different LLM architectures, and a wider range of downstream benchmarks would strengthen the findings.

*   **Potential Influence:** MASS has the potential to influence future research in several ways:
    *   It could inspire new data selection methods that leverage domain-specific knowledge structures.
    *   It provides a practical framework for improving LLMs' mathematical reasoning abilities.
    *   It highlights the importance of considering skill relationships in data selection.

**Justification for Score:**

The MASS paper presents a novel and well-executed approach to data selection for mathematical reasoning, demonstrating both improved efficiency and effectiveness in pretraining LLMs. While the reliance on a reference dataset and skill extraction model, along with limited generalizability and the relatively minor performance gain and token reduction over the original datasets temper enthusiasm, the core idea of using skill graphs to guide data selection is strong and has the potential to inspire future research. Therefore, I assign a score of 7.

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### Prada: Black-Box LLM Adaptation with Private Data on Resource-Constrained Devices
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.14932v1)
- **Authors**: Ziyao Wang, Yexiao He, Zheyu Shen, Yu Li, Guoheng Sun, Myungjin Lee, Ang Li
- **Abstract**: In recent years, Large Language Models (LLMs) have demonstrated remarkable abilities in various natural language processing tasks. However, adapting these models to specialized domains using private datasets stored on resource-constrained edge devices, such as smartphones and personal computers, remains challenging due to significant privacy concerns and limited computational resources. Existing model adaptation methods either compromise data privacy by requiring data transmission or jeopardize model privacy by exposing proprietary LLM parameters. To address these challenges, we propose Prada, a novel privacy-preserving and efficient black-box LLM adaptation system using private on-device datasets. Prada employs a lightweight proxy model fine-tuned with Low-Rank Adaptation (LoRA) locally on user devices. During inference, Prada leverages the logits offset, i.e., difference in outputs between the base and adapted proxy models, to iteratively refine outputs from a remote black-box LLM. This offset-based adaptation approach preserves both data privacy and model privacy, as there is no need to share sensitive data or proprietary model parameters. Furthermore, we incorporate speculative decoding to further speed up the inference process of Prada, making the system practically deployable on bandwidth-constrained edge devices, enabling a more practical deployment of Prada. Extensive experiments on various downstream tasks demonstrate that Prada achieves performance comparable to centralized fine-tuning methods while significantly reducing computational overhead by up to 60% and communication costs by up to 80%.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Prada: Black-Box LLM Adaptation with Private Data on Resource-Constrained Devices":

**Summary**

The paper introduces Prada, a novel system for adapting Large Language Models (LLMs) to specialized domains using private, on-device data, while addressing privacy concerns and computational resource limitations. Prada utilizes a lightweight "proxy model" fine-tuned with Low-Rank Adaptation (LoRA) locally on user devices.  During inference, Prada uses logits offsets (the difference between the base and adapted proxy model outputs) to iteratively refine the outputs of a remote black-box LLM.  The system preserves both data and model privacy as no sensitive data or LLM parameters need to be shared. Speculative decoding is integrated to accelerate inference on bandwidth-constrained devices. Experiments across various downstream tasks demonstrate that Prada achieves performance comparable to centralized fine-tuning while significantly reducing computational overhead and communication costs.

**Critical Evaluation**

* **Novelty:** The core concept of using logits offsets from a local, fine-tuned proxy model to adapt a remote black-box LLM is a noteworthy contribution.  Existing solutions typically involve either data sharing or model sharing, both with significant privacy risks.  The integration of speculative decoding is a good optimization, but the key novelty lies in the logits offset adaptation.  The idea of adapting a black-box model based on comparing its outputs to a local model, which is only possible since the output can be used to calculate a correction term. Furthermore, applying LoRA to reduce computational costs and speculative decoding can further reduce latency.
* **Significance:** The problem addressed—adapting LLMs to private, on-device data without compromising privacy or overwhelming resource-constrained devices—is highly significant.  The increasing use of LLMs on edge devices (smartphones, wearables, etc.) amplifies the need for such a solution.  If Prada, or approaches like it, proves practical and effective, it could significantly democratize access to personalized LLM services, especially in privacy-sensitive domains.  The experimental results provide a convincing initial demonstration of Prada's potential.
* **Strengths:**
    * **Privacy Preservation:**  The architecture strongly protects data privacy by keeping the training data on the device and model privacy by never exposing model parameters.
    * **Resource Efficiency:**  LoRA and the proxy model allow training and inference on resource-limited edge devices.
    * **Comprehensive Evaluation:**  The paper evaluates Prada on multiple tasks, models, and against relevant baselines (SFT, Offsite-Tuning).
    * **Practical Considerations:** Addresses the practical aspects of deployment on edge devices, considering bandwidth constraints and memory limitations.
* **Weaknesses:**
    * **Dependency on Proxy Model Quality:**  The performance of Prada is inherently linked to the quality of the proxy model. The paper acknowledges this, but a more in-depth analysis of the relationship between proxy model characteristics (size, architecture, pre-training data) and overall system performance would be beneficial.
    * **Latency Trade-offs:** While speculative decoding helps, the iterative client-server communication inherent in Prada introduces latency. While Prada-Transfer offers the best trade-off, it needs transferal of proxy model which might be infeasible.
    * **Limited Comparison with FL:**  While the paper mentions federated learning, a more direct comparison, particularly in terms of communication cost and privacy leakage, would strengthen the argument for Prada's superiority. This is especially relevant since FL is also a technique that aims to minimize the sharing of model or data to the server.
    * **Inference Cost:** The paper mentions inference cost is low; however, no actual number is provided, making it hard to quantify the efficiency of the implementation.
* **Potential Influence:** Prada has the potential to influence future research in privacy-preserving machine learning, federated learning, and edge AI. It provides a viable alternative to data-sharing or model-sharing approaches, particularly in scenarios where privacy and resource constraints are paramount. It also showcases the effectiveness of logits-based adaptation methods.
* **Rigorous rationale:** The system manages to keep both the training data of the on-device model private, and also avoid revealing any of the weights to the user, preventing model theft. The communication cost is also improved due to LoRA and the reduced proxy model compared to previous iterations. However, these gains are not without cost, as the introduction of the additional models can increase latency and require server support to support the proxy model.

**Score: 7**

**Justification:** Prada presents a novel and significant approach to adapting LLMs on resource-constrained devices while preserving privacy, meriting a high score. While the paper has some weaknesses, the idea of using proxy-model logits offsets for adaptation is clever and well-supported by experiments. The impact on the field could be substantial as it unlocks the potential for personalized LLM services on edge devices in a secure and efficient manner. While the latency trade-offs can impact real-world usage, and should be addressed further, the current state of research definitely calls for additional efforts in this direction.

- **Classification**: cs.CR
- **Score**: 7/10

### FAVOR-Bench: A Comprehensive Benchmark for Fine-Grained Video Motion Understanding
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.14935v1)
- **Authors**: Chongjun Tu, Lin Zhang, Pengtao Chen, Peng Ye, Xianfang Zeng, Wei Cheng, Gang Yu, Tao Chen
- **Abstract**: Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs) have shown remarkable capabilities in video content understanding but still struggle with fine-grained motion comprehension. To comprehensively assess the motion understanding ability of existing MLLMs, we introduce FAVOR-Bench, comprising 1,776 videos with structured manual annotations of various motions. Our benchmark includes both close-ended and open-ended tasks. For close-ended evaluation, we carefully design 8,184 multiple-choice question-answer pairs spanning six distinct sub-tasks. For open-ended evaluation, we develop both a novel cost-efficient LLM-free and a GPT-assisted caption assessment method, where the former can enhance benchmarking interpretability and reproducibility. Comprehensive experiments with 21 state-of-the-art MLLMs reveal significant limitations in their ability to comprehend and describe detailed temporal dynamics in video motions. To alleviate this limitation, we further build FAVOR-Train, a dataset consisting of 17,152 videos with fine-grained motion annotations. The results of finetuning Qwen2.5-VL on FAVOR-Train yield consistent improvements on motion-related tasks of TVBench, MotionBench and our FAVOR-Bench. Comprehensive assessment results demonstrate that the proposed FAVOR-Bench and FAVOR-Train provide valuable tools to the community for developing more powerful video understanding models. Project page: \href{https://favor-bench.github.io/}{https://favor-bench.github.io/}.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "FAVOR-Bench: A Comprehensive Benchmark for Fine-Grained Video Motion Understanding":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces FAVOR-Bench, a new benchmark designed to evaluate the fine-grained motion understanding capabilities of Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs). The benchmark contains 1,776 videos annotated with structured motion information, enabling both close-ended (multiple-choice QA) and open-ended evaluation tasks.  The authors also propose a novel LLM-free evaluation framework for open-ended tasks to reduce cost and enhance interpretability. The paper presents comprehensive experiments with 21 state-of-the-art MLLMs, revealing limitations in their ability to comprehend and describe detailed temporal dynamics in video motions.  Finally, the authors create FAVOR-Train, a dataset of 17,152 videos with fine-grained motion annotations, and demonstrate that fine-tuning MLLMs on this dataset improves performance on motion-related tasks, including those in FAVOR-Bench, TVBench, and MotionBench.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's primary novelty lies in the creation of FAVOR-Bench, specifically designed to address the under-explored area of fine-grained video motion understanding.  While existing benchmarks touch on action recognition or event-level understanding, FAVOR-Bench goes a step further by focusing on detailed temporal dynamics within actions and camera movements. The LLM-free evaluation framework, though not groundbreaking, presents a practical contribution towards making the evaluation of generative MLLMs more accessible and interpretable. The FAVOR-Train dataset is also a novel contribution tailored to address a specific identified weakness.

*   **Significance:** The significance of this work stems from the growing importance of video understanding in various applications, including robotics, embodied AI, and video generation. Identifying limitations in current MLLMs' ability to handle fine-grained motion understanding is crucial for guiding future research and development. The benchmark and training dataset offer concrete resources for researchers to build and assess improved models.  The experiments conducted are thorough and provide valuable insights into the strengths and weaknesses of a range of MLLMs. The improvements demonstrated by fine-tuning on FAVOR-Train also indicate a clear path toward better video understanding models.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   The benchmark dataset is carefully curated and annotated with a clear focus on fine-grained motion details.
    *   The inclusion of both close-ended and open-ended evaluation tasks provides a comprehensive assessment framework.
    *   The LLM-free evaluation method is a practical contribution that can enhance reproducibility and reduce the cost of evaluating generative models.
    *   The construction of the FAVOR-Train dataset directly addresses the identified limitations in MLLMs.
    *   The experimental evaluation is extensive and includes a variety of state-of-the-art MLLMs.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   While the LLM-free evaluation framework is a good step towards reducing evaluation cost, its reliance on semantic similarity and pre-trained models also potentially limits its sensitivity to nuances and creativity in model outputs. The effectiveness of the framework likely depends heavily on the quality and representativeness of the pre-trained models used.
    *   Although the paper does an extensive evaluation of 21 models, the conclusion is fairly common. The conclusion that MLLMs still struggle with fine-grained details has been established with earlier research. This limits the paper's findings.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence the field by:

    *   Directing research efforts toward improving MLLMs' ability to understand and describe fine-grained video motions.
    *   Providing a valuable benchmark and training dataset for researchers to develop and evaluate new models.
    *   Encouraging the development of more accessible and interpretable evaluation methods for generative MLLMs.

* **Rigorous Rationale:** The paper provides a comprehensive video benchmark tailored specifically towards fine-grained motion understanding. The paper effectively shows the gap in ability of SOTA models and introduces an LLM-free evaluation method to reduce the cost. These factors, along with the weakness of the conclusions being limited, and the potential for influence in future research warrant a score of:

**Score: 7.5**

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### Proceedings of the 3rd Italian Conference on Big Data and Data Science (ITADATA2024)
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.14937v1)
- **Authors**: Nicola Bena, Claudia Diamantini, Michela Natilli, Luigi Romano, Giovanni Stilo, Valentina Pansanella, Claudio A. Ardagna, Anna Monreale, Roberto Trasarti
- **Abstract**: Proceedings of the 3rd Italian Conference on Big Data and Data Science (ITADATA2024), held in Pisa, Italy, September 17-19, 2024. The Italian Conference on Big Data and Data Science (ITADATA2024) is the annual event supported by the CINI Big Data National Laboratory and ISTI CNR that aims to put together Italian researchers and professionals from academia, industry, government, and public administration working in the field of big data and data science, as well as related fields (e.g., security and privacy, HPC, Cloud). ITADATA2024 covered research on all theoretical and practical aspects of Big Data and data science including data governance, data processing, data analysis, data reporting, data protection, as well as experimental studies and lessons learned. In particular, ITADATA2024 focused on - Data spaces - Data processing life cycle - Machine learning and Large Language Models - Applications of big data and data science in healthcare, finance, industry 5.0, and beyond - Data science for social network analysis
- **Summary**: ## Summary of ITADATA2024 Proceedings

The ITADATA2024 conference brought together Italian researchers and professionals to discuss advancements in Big Data and Data Science. The conference covered theoretical and practical aspects, including data governance, processing, analysis, reporting, and protection. Key focus areas included Data Spaces, the Data Processing Life Cycle, Machine Learning and Large Language Models, applications in various sectors like healthcare, finance, and industry 5.0, and Data Science for Social Network Analysis.

## Novelty and Significance Evaluation of ITADATA2024 Proceedings

This is a summary of the *proceedings* of a conference, not a single paper. Evaluating the novelty and significance is therefore inherently more complex and must be done at a higher level.

**Strengths:**

*   **Comprehensive Coverage:** The proceedings cover a wide range of relevant and contemporary topics within Big Data and Data Science, demonstrating the breadth of research being conducted in Italy. The inclusion of topics like Data Spaces and Industry 5.0 applications suggests awareness of emerging trends.
*   **Community Building:** ITADATA2024 serves as a valuable platform for fostering collaboration and knowledge sharing within the Italian research community and between academia, industry, and government. This is crucial for driving innovation and practical application.
*   **Focus on Applications:** The emphasis on applications in healthcare, finance, industry 5.0, and social network analysis indicates a commitment to translating research into tangible benefits and addressing real-world challenges. The focus on these application domains is timely and relevant.
*   **Italian Focus:** The conference provides a specific window into the advancements and challenges within the Italian context, which may be of interest to those seeking to understand the national landscape of Big Data and Data Science research.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Lack of Granularity:** As proceedings, the content's depth and novelty are uneven. Some individual papers might be highly innovative, while others could be incremental improvements or replications of existing work. Without examining each paper individually, it's impossible to assess the true significance of the collection as a whole.
*   **Potential for Duplication:** Conference proceedings often include work-in-progress or preliminary findings that might later be published in more prestigious journals. There's a potential for overlap with previously published research.
*   **Limited Global Impact:** While fostering the Italian community is important, the conference's impact on the global Big Data and Data Science landscape may be limited compared to more internationally renowned events. The proceedings are primarily targeted toward the Italian context.
*   **No Mention of specific advances:** The description only covers the topic areas and doesn't go into detail on any notable or novel advances made. This makes it harder to judge how much these topics were furthered in the conference.

**Justification:**

Considering the above, the ITADATA2024 proceedings represent a valuable contribution to the Italian research community, facilitating collaboration and showcasing national research efforts in key areas of Big Data and Data Science. The breadth of topics and focus on applications are positive indicators. However, the lack of granular information about the specific contributions of individual papers, the potential for overlap with existing literature, and the primarily national focus limit its overall impact on the global research community. A purely local conference would be in the lower range, but since it included topics of international relevance, it's pushed up slightly.

**Score: 5**

- **Score**: 5/10

### VisNumBench: Evaluating Number Sense of Multimodal Large Language Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.14939v1)
- **Authors**: Tengjin Weng, Jingyi Wang, Wenhao Jiang, Zhong Ming
- **Abstract**: Can Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs) develop an intuitive number sense similar to humans? Targeting this problem, we introduce Visual Number Benchmark (VisNumBench) to evaluate the number sense abilities of MLLMs across a wide range of visual numerical tasks. VisNumBench consists of about 1,900 multiple-choice question-answer pairs derived from both synthetic and real-world visual data, covering seven visual numerical attributes and four types of visual numerical estimation tasks. Our experiments on VisNumBench led to the following key findings: (i) The 17 MLLMs we tested, including open-source models such as Qwen2.5-VL and InternVL2.5, as well as proprietary models like GPT-4o and Gemini 2.0 Flash, perform significantly below human levels in number sense-related tasks. (ii) Multimodal mathematical models and multimodal chain-of-thought (CoT) models did not exhibit significant improvements in number sense abilities. (iii) Stronger MLLMs with larger parameter sizes and broader general abilities demonstrate modest gains in number sense abilities. We believe VisNumBench will serve as a valuable resource for the research community, encouraging further advancements in enhancing MLLMs' number sense abilities. All benchmark resources, including code and datasets, will be publicly available at https://wwwtttjjj.github.io/VisNumBench/.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "VisNumBench: Evaluating Number Sense of Multimodal Large Language Models":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces VisNumBench, a new benchmark designed to evaluate the "number sense" abilities of Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs).  VisNumBench consists of approximately 1900 question-answer pairs derived from both synthetic and real-world visual data. The benchmark covers seven visual numerical attributes (angle, scale, length, quantity, depth, area, and volume) and four types of visual numerical estimation tasks (range estimation, value comparison, value estimation, and multiplicative estimation). The authors tested 17 MLLMs on VisNumBench, including both open-source and proprietary models, and found that their performance significantly lags behind human-level capabilities in number sense-related tasks.  The study also explored the impact of multimodal mathematical models and chain-of-thought (CoT) techniques, finding that they don't significantly improve number sense abilities.  Finally, the authors observe that stronger MLLMs with larger parameter sizes show modest improvements in number sense. The benchmark resources, including code and datasets, are planned to be publicly available.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The key novelty lies in explicitly focusing on "number sense" as a specific cognitive ability to be evaluated in MLLMs. While existing benchmarks assess mathematical reasoning and numerical interpretation, VisNumBench aims to tap into the intuitive and approximate numerical estimation that humans perform effortlessly.
